12220 EFEA C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12221 EFEC 0450  20 	b *r0
12222               	;
12223               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12224               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12225               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12226               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12227               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12228               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12229               	; scan_wipe:
12230               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12231               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12232               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12233               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12234               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12235               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12236               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12237               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12238               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12239               	; 	'
12240               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12241               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12242               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12243               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12244               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12245               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12246               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12247               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12248               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12249               	; 	'
12250               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12251               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12252               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12253               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12254               	; 	'
12255               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12256               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12257               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12258 EFEE 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EFF0 1680     
12259 EFF2 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     EFF4 20E0

12325               	;
12326               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12327               	; 	'
12328               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12329               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12330               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12331               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12332               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12333               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12334               	; 	'
12335               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12336               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12337               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12338               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12339               	; 	'
12340               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12341               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12342               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12343               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12344               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12345               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12346               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12347               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12348               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12349               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12350               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

13140               	;
13141               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13142               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13143               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13144               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13145               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13146               	; 	'
13147               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13148               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13149               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.

13154               	; 	'
13155               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable

13204               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS TALLY B -- a blip that COUNTS rather than a buzz that
13205               	; 	' ratchets. Two frames on and two off at 1,036 Hz (divisor 108), against
13206               	; 	' the 373 Hz it was: the pitch is what separates a count from the
13207               	; 	' timer's own low tick, and the shorter gap makes ten of them a run
13208               	; 	' rather than a queue.
13209               	; 	'
13210               	; 	' Channel 2 stays. The effect table uses it for the upper voice of a
13211               	; 	' two-note effect, so a tick here cannot cancel a sustained tone on

     F782 F6D4     
13267               	;
13268               	; 	' AND IT ENDS ON ITS LAST BLIP -- there is no closing accent.
13269               	; 	'
13270               	; 	' Two were tried. The bench's own ending for this variant rose a fourth,
13271               	; 	' to 2,542 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek -- the same fault the
13272               	; 	' pickup was rewritten for. Dropping it an OCTAVE BELOW the ticks, to
13273               	; 	' 518 Hz, was reported the same way, and that one cannot be too high by
13274               	; 	' any measure: it is the lowest note in the effect.
13275               	; 	'
13276               	; 	' So the complaint is not the pitch, it is the extra note. A tally is
13277               	; 	' arithmetic, and arithmetic finishes when the last term is added; a
13278               	; 	' flourish after it is a second event the player has to interpret, and
13279               	; 	' the HUD has already said the clock is empty. The count stops when the
13280               	; 	' counting stops.
13281               	;
13282               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13283               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13284               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13285               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13286               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13287               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13288               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13289               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13290               	; 	'
13291               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13292               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13293               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13294               	;
13295               	; do_escape:
13296               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13297               	; 	escapd = 0
13298 F784 04C0  14 	clr r0
13299 F786 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCAPD
     F788 2112     
13300               	; 	rsn = 0
13301 F78A 04C0  14 	clr r0

     F78E 220A

13334 F7C4 EA98     	data cvb_HIDE_ALL
13335               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13336               	; 	'
13337               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13338               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13339               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13340               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13341               	; 	'
13342               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13343               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13344               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13345               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
13346               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
13347               	; 	'
13348               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
13349               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
13350               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
13351               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
13352               	; 	'
13353               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
13354               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
13355               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
13356               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
13357               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
13358               	; 	' program uses a constant.
13359               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "
13360 F7C6 0200  20 	li r0,330
     F7C8 014A

     F7CC 8342

     F7D4 000D

13522               	;
13523               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13524               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13525               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13526               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13527               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13528               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13529               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13530               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13531               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13532               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13533               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13534               	; snd_off:
13535               cvb_SND_OFF
13536               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL sfx_tick CAN WRITE, AND CHANNEL 3 IS ONE OF THEM. This
13537               	; 	' routine was written when the footstep was a pair of tones on channel 0
13538               	; 	' and it still named only 0, 1 and 2 after the footstep moved to the
13539               	; 	' NOISE channel. So a step ringing when Harry was caught was never turned
13540               	; 	' off: it hissed through the whole bonus tally and did not stop until the
13541               	; 	' next round's first footstep happened to reset it.
13542               	; 	'
13543               	; 	' Zeroing sot does NOT silence it. The counter only emits its note-off on
13544               	; 	' the pass it reaches zero, so setting it to zero by hand skips the very
13545               	; 	' write that would have stopped the sound.
13546               	; 	SOUND 0,0,0

13713               cv966
13714               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13715               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13716               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13717               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13718               	; 	'
13719               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13720               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13721               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13722               	; 	'
13723               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13724               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13725               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13726               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13727               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.
13728               	; 	IF sfp = 1 THEN
13729 FA94 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFP,r0
     FA96 2204     
13730 FA98 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     FA9A 0100

     FAA2 FAB2

     FAB0 2228

13742               cv968
13743               	; 	' ONE CHANNEL, WALKED THROUGH BY A COUNTDOWN. sfx_tick runs once a pass at
13744               	; 	' about 42 ms, so thirteen passes is the bench's 550 ms; the note and the
13745               	; 	' volume are chosen from the count rather than stored in a table, which
13746               	; 	' for six phases is smaller than the table would be.
13747               	; 	'
13748               	; 	' Divisors: C5 523 Hz = 214, E5 659 = 170, G5 784 = 143.

13898               	;
13899               	; 	' the jump sweep: divisor falling = pitch rising
13900               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
13901               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
13902               	; 	'
13903               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
13904               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
13905               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
13906               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
13907               	; 	' recording shows.
13908               	; 	'
13909               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
13910               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
13911               	; 	' is written).
13912               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN
13913 FC1A D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FC1C 2094

13968               cv990
13969               	;
13970               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
13971               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
13972               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
13973               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
13974               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
13975               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
13976               	;
13977               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
13978               	; 	'
13979               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
13980               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
13981               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
13982               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
13983               	; 	'
13984               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
13985               	; 	'
13986               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
13987               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
13988               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
13989               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
13990               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
13991               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
13992               	; 	'
13993               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
13994               	; 	'
13995               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
13996               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
13997               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
13998               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
13999               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
14000               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
14001               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
14002               	; 	'
14003               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
14004               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
14005               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
14006               	; 	' carry is wrong.
14007               	; 	'
14008               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
14009               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
14010               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
14011               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
14012               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
14013               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
14014               	; 	'
14015               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
14016               	; 	'
14017               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
14018               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
14019               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
14020               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
14021               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
14022               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
14023               	; 	' back to 7.
14024               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN
14025 FCA8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FCAA 2208     
14026 FCAC 0201  20 	li r1,3840   ; 15*256
     FCAE 0F00

     FD74 FDA4     
14122               cv1009
14123               	; 		spt = spt - 1
14124 FD76 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SPT,r0
     FD78 2226     
14125 FD7A 0220  22 	ai r0,65280	; -1
     FD7C FF00     
14126 FD7E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SPT
     FD80 2226     
14127               	; 		IF spt = 0 THEN SOUND 2,0,0
14128 FD82 04C1  14 	clr r1
14129 FD84 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
14130 FD86 1302  14 	jeq cv1011
14131 FD88 0460  28 	b @cv1010
     FD8A FDA4     
14132               cv1011
14133 FD8C 04C0  14 	clr r0
14134 FD8E 0202  20 	li r2,>c000
     FD90 C000     
14135 FD92 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FD94 A980     
14136 FD96 A2BA     	data sn76489_freq
14137 FD98 04C0  14 	clr r0
14138 FD9A 0202  20 	li r2,>d000
     FD9C D000     
14139 FD9E 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FDA0 A980     
14140 FDA2 A2D6     	data sn76489_vol
14141               cv1010
14142               	; 	END IF
14143               cv1008
14144               	; 	RETURN
14145 FDA4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
14146 FDA6 0450  20 	b *r0
14147               	;
14148               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
14149               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
14150               	; 	'
14151               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
14152               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
14153               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
14154               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
14155               	; 	'
14156               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
14157               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
14158               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
14159               	; 	'
14160               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
14161               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
14162               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
14163               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
14164               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
14165               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
14166               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
14167               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
14168               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14169               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14170               	; 	#if TI994A
14171               	; 	BANK 1
14172      0256     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

14181               	; 	'
14182               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
14183               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
14184               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
14185               	; 	'
14186               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
14187               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
14188               	; 	' letter after it.
14189               	;

14310               	;
14311               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
14312               	; 	'
14313               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
14314               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
14315               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
14316               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
14317               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
14318               	; 	'
14319               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
14320               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.
14321               	;
14322               	; font_col:
14323               cvb_FONT_COL

14442               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14443               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14444               	; 	'
14445               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14446               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14447               	; 	' being small sprites.
14448               	; 	'
14449               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14450               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14451               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14452               	; 	'
14453               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14454               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14455               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14456               	;
14457               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14458               cvb_SPR_KELLY
14459               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

15458               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15459               	;
15460               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15461               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15462               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15463               	; 	' unreadable.
15464               	;
15465               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

15577               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15578               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15579               	; 	'
15580               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15581               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15582               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15583               	; 	'
15584               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15585               	; 	' blit is
15586               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15587               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15588               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15589               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15590               	; 	'
15591               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15592               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15593               	; 	'
15594               	; 	' template ids:
15595               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15596               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15597               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15598               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15599               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15600               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15601               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15602               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15603               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15604               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15605               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15606               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15607               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15608               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15609               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15610               	;
15611               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15612               cvb_STOR_TPL
